Potential algorithms for side-only nonnegative:

**Frank-Wolfe / subgradient on simplex**

Normalize sum=1. Objective F(a) = max_{k=1}^{n-1} C_k(a) where C_k = sum_i a_i a_{i+k} (for nonnegative lags only; symmetric same). Minimize G(a)=C_peak. Score = 2n G (for sum1). Need min G ~0.75/n. c0 can be > G. In fact a can be concentrated.

At current a, active lag k* maximizing side. Gradient of C_{k*} w.r.t a_i: 2*shift of a by k*: grad_i = a_i a_{i+k} energy derivative = 2 * (a_{i-k} + a_{i+k}) with boundaries (for positive lag). More generally for both ±k, C_k should count both lags: C_k = sum_i a_i a_{i+k}; convolution symmetric so one side enough. If max over all nonzero lags, derivative of C_k = 2*(shift left k + shift right k) (within bounds). We can choose descent direction v = -grad a_k, Frank-Wolfe linear minimizer: min_{s in simplex} <grad, s> over simplex = choose coordinate with minimal grad? Since simplex {a_i>=0,sum=1}; linear term with coefficient grad_j; min at mass 1 at min grad coordinate. Frank-Wolfe step: a_new = (1-t)a + t e_{j_min}, where j is coordinate with smallest gradient component. That means move mass to low contribution coordinate. Gradient components for active lag k: grad_i = 2(a_{i-k}+a_{i+k}) (or one side). Points where neighbors at distance k are low have low gradient. This tends to spread mass to positions not near existing mass at shift k. Similar to greedy construction of low-autocorrelation sequences. Could be effective. For multiple active lags, use weighted gradient sum.

Project gradient methods: Since objective nonconvex but smooth at max, can use softmax weights: p over lags ∝ exp(C_k/T); gradient = weighted sum. Mirror descent (multiplicative weights) may work better for nonnegative. Previous used mirror descent. We can adapt with side lags and active smooth max. For probability vector sum 1, update multiplicative: a_i <- a_i exp(-eta grad_i) / sum. This decreases lags where a_i has high interacting neighbors. It can be implemented FFT. This is easy and differentiable. We can tune eta, temperature T.

For side-only, gradient through softmax:
- Compute C(lags 1..n-1) via autocorr. Exclude center.
- p_k = softmax(C/T) over side lags (maybe weights over ± same diag no matter).
- grad_i = 2 ∑_{k=1}^{n-1} p_k (a_{i-k}+a_{i+k}) with zero out of bounds. This can be computed via convolution of p with a for each side: valid convolution? p length n-1. We can compute `corr_off = np.correlate(p, a, 'same')`? Need vector derivative. If we define C_k = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-k} a_i a_{i+k}. Derivative wrt a_j = ∑_{k} p_k (for term a_j a_{j+k} if j+k<n: p_k + if j-k>=0: p_k). So grad = 2*(convolve(p, a, 'valid')? Let's derive:
  left contribution h_j = ∑_{k=1}^{n-1-j} p_k a_{j+k}. This is cross-correlation of a with p at lags. right contribution = ∑_{k=1}^{j} p_k a_{j-k}.

We can compute via FFT: full cross-correlation r = correlate(a, a) full. For weighted p, need sum p_k (a shifted). Equivalent to sequence b where b_k = p_k for k>0 and b_{-k}=p_k. Then grad = 2 * (b * a)(mod? linear) restricted to length n using valid? More simply, `temp = correlate(a, p, mode='valid')`? If p length n-1, a length n, correlate returns length n? Let's test mentally: np.correlate(a, p, 'valid') returns length n (n-(n-1)+1=n) at lags 0..n-1: for result[j] = ∑_{i} a[i+j] p[i]? It gives one side. We need both sides. Could set `grad_right = correlate(a, p, mode='valid')` maybe; left is reverse? Or compute via FFT convolution:
Let rev_p = p reversed? We want for each j, sum_{k=1}^{n-1} p_k a_{j-k} + p_k a_{j+k}. The first is cross-correlation at lag k. This is convolution of a with kernel h where h[k]=p_k, h[-k]=p_k, h[0]=0, truncated linear (not circular). If we do linear convolution of a with full h (length 2n-1), then keep positions where complete? But terms outside sequence should not contribute. If h symmetric length 2n-1 with zeros at center, and convolve a (length n) with h (length 2n-1) gives length 3n-2; we need values for j=0..n-1 where both a within and kernel offsets -n..n? Since h defined for offsets -n+1..n-1. Linear convolution: (h*a)_j = ∑_i h_{i} a_{i-j}? Need align. Easier to use direct formula with shifts maybe O(n^2) too slow for many iterations. But we can approximate via circular FFT if boundary effects small, using periodic sequence? The actual convolution is aperiodic; extrapolate zeros. For large n, boundary negligible? For optimization, we can use circular autocorrelation and gradient; may improve cyclic side peaks but not aperiodic? The problem uses aperiodic convolution. Boundary effects could matter, but sequence length chosen, maybe periodic properties good.

Can compute exact gradient for weighted p using FFT in O(n log n): Let p_ext length L. Define array `k` of length L=2n-1 where k[n-1]=0, k[n-1+k]=p_k, k[n-1-k]=p_k. Then gradient m = correlate? Actually C_k = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-k} a_i a_{i+k} = dot(a[0:n-k], a[k:n]). Derivative = v_k (a shifted down) + v_k (a shifted up). Weighted sum = convolve? The operation ∑_k p_k shift_{-k} a + shift_k a. This is (S p) * where S_k shifts. It's a linear convolution of a with h of length 2n-1 but keep central n positions: (h * a)[n-1+j] = ∑_{t=0}^{n-1} h[n-1+j-t] a_t. For t in 0..n-1, h index n-1+j-t. We need terms where offset = (n-1+j-t) - (n-1) = j-t. Thus h[n-1 + (j-t)]. Sum over t: ∑_t h[n-1 + j - t] a_t. If h offset d = n-1+index. Set d = t - j? For term a_t a_{t±k}: offset? def _next_pow2(m): ...
```
def _prepare_sequence(seq, n=None): ... returns np array nonnegative finite, normalized sum=1 if sum>0 else uniform.
```

```
    except Exception:
       return np.inf

```

If subtract min, high m relative to min reduced. If m all equal, no change. We can use `delta = m - m.mean()` so some increase some decrease. The constant doesn't matter after normalization, but subtracting mean makes step balanced and can increase low m. Without mean, all masses decrease; after normalization, those with smaller m increase relative. Equivalent to subtracting min? Actually after normalization, `a_i exp(-eta m_i) / sum`. This is same as subtracting min: `a_i exp(-eta (m_i - min)) / sum exp`. So relative low m increase relative to min. We can compute `z = m - m.max()` for numerical, no mean needed. **Candidate update formula**:
```
def _mirror_update(a, m, eta):
   a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
   z = m - m.max()
   # optionally center z: z -= z.mean() to allow increase above original? normalizing already; but z max 0, exp <=1, then some normalized may increase if others drop more. Good.
   new = a_safe * np.exp(eta * z)  # since z<=0. Equivalent to exp(-eta(m-max)).
   new = np.maximum(new, floor)
   new /= new.sum()
   return new
```
But if many m=max, they drop; low m unchanged relative, so increase. Good.

If m has broad max plateau, all plateau drop. Fine.

**Line search exact side peak**:

Pseudo:
```
def _autocorr_pos(a):
 n=len(a); M=1<<((2*n-1).bit_length()); F=np.fft.rfft(a,M); ac=np.fft.irfft(np.abs(F)**2, M)[:n].real; return ac
```
For n=6121, M=16384. Good.

`_side_peak(a, ac=None): if ac None ac=_autocorr_pos(a); return max(ac[1:]) if n>1 else 0.0`

`_side_gradient(a, T, c=None):`
- if c None c=_autocorr_pos(a); side=c[1:]; peak=side.max(); if peak <=0: m=zeros
- if T<=0 or peak<=0: p=zeros; p[argmax side]=1
- else: shifted=side - peak; p=exp(shifted/T); p/=sum.
- kernel length M_grad. We need center = n-1. Create kernel as np.zeros(M_grad). For d=1..n-1, w=p[d-1]; kernel[center + d] = w; kernel[center - d] = w. But indices center-d may be 0..n-1; for d=n-1, center-d=0. center+d=2n-2. Both < M. Good.
- FFT: F_a=np.fft.rfft(a, M_grad); F_k=np.fft.rfft(kernel, M_grad); conv=np.fft.irfft(F_a*F_k, M_grad).real; m=conv[center:center+n]. Could conv have slight negative? clip m >=0 because a,p nonnegative; numerical maybe.
- To reduce cost, we can compute autocorr c and gradient in one pass? We need p; separate. Fine.
- Return m, c.

Function `_local_pair_polish(a, deadline, rng)`:

   c = autocorr_pos(a); side=c[1:]; peak=side.max(); k=int(argmax(side))+1

```
def _local_direct(a, deadline, rng):
   a = a.copy(); a = floor normalize
   C = _autocorr_pos(a)[1:]  # length n-1
   peak = C.max()
   best_proxy=peak; best_actual=eval
   for it in range(...):
      # update peak index lazily: maintain peak? After C update, max can be computed O(n)
      k = int(np.argmax(C)) + 1
      # contribution vector for lag k: prod = a[:n-k]*a[k:]
      # choose max product index
      idx = int(np.argmax(prod))
      i = idx; j = idx+k
      # source = i if a[i]>a[j] else j; source should have enough mass
      # target selection: choose t not source; maybe from set with low `a[t]` and low active interactions
      # Compute interaction score for active top R lags: for a coordinate t, grad active = sum_{r in top} (a[t-r]+a[t+r])
      # We can maintain active weights top R=5 with weights maybe C sorted.
      # To avoid O(Rn) computing for all t, we can approximate via C? Or build active m via shifts for R lags (R=4) O(Rn) using vectorized arrays.
      active_lags = top maybe [k, k2...]
      m_active = zeros(n)
      for l in active_lags[:R]:
          m_active[:n-l] += a[l:]
          m_active[l:] += a[:-l]
      # target candidates: choose indices with smallest m_active, maybe excluding source and j, with random tie.
      # Also maybe require a_t < a_source? Not necessary.
      # Generate candidate moves: for fraction list, exact update C_trial = C.copy() + ...
      # For each target (top 5 low m) and fractions, update and check max. Choose best.
      if not improved: noise or change active weights.
```

**Time for each `_optimize_side`**:
Function arguments: `a, deadline, rng, name, eval_every=20, max_it=None`.
Pseudo:
```
def _optimize_side(a, deadline, rng, name, eval_every=15, eta_init=0.35, tau_start=0.35, tau_end=0.005):
   a = _prepare(a)
   c = autocorr_pos(a); peak = max(c[1:])
   actual = safe_eval(a)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_actual=actual; best_proxy=peak
   if not finite actual, actual=proxy_score
   T = max(peak*tau_start, 1e-18)
   it=0; stuck=0; eta=eta_init
   n=len(a)
   while time+0.05 < deadline:
       it +=1
       # progress; tau = tau_start*(tau_end/tau_start)**progress
       # need total iterations estimate? time based: tau = current based on time elapsed / job_budget? We can pass job_start and budget.
       # Simpler: use iterations up to max 20000; tau = tau_start*(tau_end/tau_start)**(it/10000).
       c, p, m = _side_gradient(a, T, c)
       mscale = m.max()
       if mscale < 1e-300: break
       improved=False
       # try eta sequence around current
       for e in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25, eta*2.0]: maybe unique positive up to <3
           new = _mirror_update(a, m, e)
           newc = autocorr_pos(new); np_peak = max(newc[1:])
           if np_peak < peak - 1e-14:
               a=new; c=newc; peak=np_peak; improved=True
               # increase eta slowly
               eta = min(2.0, eta*1.15)
               break
           # keep best_trial if close?
       if not improved:
           # try accept if proxy smooth? could do smaller eta 0.1; if still no, perturb
           e=0.01...
           if no: stuck +=1; if stuck>3: random perturb; T = T*0.5? eta=eta*0.5
       else stuck=0
       # periodically eval actual
       if it%eval_every==0 or peak < best_proxy*0.999:
           sc=safe_eval(a); if finite and sc<best_actual: update; best_proxy=peak
           if sc < best_actual - ...:
       if peak <= best_proxy*(1-1e-6): maybe eval
   # final eval
   return best_a, best_actual
```

We need compute `_side_gradient` after we have c from previous accepted. It returns m and p. We can use c to avoid recompute.

But for each eta trial, we compute autocorr of new; expensive. Limit to 2-3 etas. Perhaps use a single eta annealed, no line search, with proxy check each iteration. Previous line search may be overkill. But line search improves. Let's design efficient:
- Use eta schedule from 0.6 down to 0.02. Compute one candidate each iteration. If peak doesn't improve, reduce eta; if improves, retain/increase. That's like adaptive. We already do.
- We can evaluate new peak for candidate; if no improve, we can try eta*0.5 (one extra FFT). So max 2-3.

The temperature tau: With side c, sometimes `c` differences are very small; T too high gives smooth. We can set tau based on current peak and rank: `T = peak * (0.5 * (tau_end/tau_start)**progress)`. But if peak decreases, T decreases. Good.

To escape local minima:
- If many stuck (no proxy improvement for `stuck_limit=8` candidate evals), do:
   * revert to best_a if current much worse? Since we accept only improvements, current never worse. Stuck means no improvement from current best. We can perturb current: add noise `a = 0.95*a + 0.05*rand`, normalize; recompute peak. It may increase proxy; but we accept for exploration. Set best still.
   * or take a random top 10% coordinates and equalize.
- We could also run from a pulled vector `0.7 best + 0.3 random` with a few mirror steps and if improves accept. This is expensive; maybe not.

Since local polish will also explore.

**Local direct function details and correctness**:
We need `C` exact positive lags. Use `_autocorr_pos(a)` returns ac length n; C=ac[1:]. For nonnegative, positive lags equal negative. Good.
For coordinate gradient `g_x` array length n-1:
```
def _coord_grad_for_lags(a, x, out=None):
    n = len(a); L=n-1
    if out is None: out=np.zeros(L)
    else: out.fill(0.0)
    if x+1 < n:
        out[:n-x-1] = a[x+1:n]
    if x > 0:
        out[:x] = a[x-1::-1]
    return out
```
Check x=0: plus a[1:] length n-1 assigned; minus none. For lag l=1, grad a_1, correct. x=n-1: plus none; minus a[n-2::-1] length n-1: lag1 a[n-2], lag n-1 a[0], correct.

For exact candidate update from s to t with delta:
```
def _try_move(a, C, s, t, delta):
   if delta<=0 or s==t: return None
   if a[s] < delta: delta = a[s]
   gt = _coord_grad_for_lags(a,t, buf_t)
   gs = _coord_grad_for_lags(a,s, buf_s)
   newC = C + delta*(gt - gs)
   d=abs(t-s)
   if 1 <= d <= n-1:
       newC[d-1] -= delta*delta
   # need revert exact for terms where both endpoints? Are there cases where g_t and g_s include a_s/a_t twice? We accounted product once. But what if t and s are paired for lag d in both plus and minus arrays? For distance d, in g_t one of plus/minus includes a_s; in g_s the other includes a_t. Not both in same g. So one -δ^2.
   newC = np.maximum(newC, 0) ? Mathematically nonnegative but if clipping could hide. For max, don't clip until after; but small negative due round. Use np.maximum for peak? It might overestimate if negative? peak max unaffected if max positive. Use if newC.min()< -1e-12: recompute? For peak, we can use `np.max(newC)`; if all negative impossible. We'll store clipped for future? Better store as is then clip after update: C = np.maximum(newC, 0)
   peak = float(np.max(newC))
   return newC, peak
```
But exact update formula using first-order gradients plus -δ^2 is exact for all lags because C is bilinear; derivative vector g uses original a, and the pair cross term only when s,t paired. Need confirm for lags where both s and t are *both possible*? Suppose l=|t-s|, but if t-s = l, pair term. If l maybe also another term? In a 1D sequence, each pair at distance l is unique: indices (i,i+l). Could s and t be paired with each other in two ways? No.
What about if t is such that `t+l` and `t-l` include s? only one because l positive.
Good.

Need update C for multiple moves? We're just testing; if accept, set C=newC.

Target selection: To choose low m_active, we can use C top lags. But if top lags have weights, we can build `m_active` for top R lags and `resp`. We can reuse `buf_m` and `buf_resp`. Implementation:
```
def _select_move_candidates(a, C, R=5, Ktargets=12, rng=None):
    n=len(a); L=n-1
    top = np.argsort(C)[-R:] + 1  # lags
    m_act = np.zeros(n)
    resp = np.zeros(n)
    for l in top:
       l=int(l)
       m_act[:n-l] += a[l:]
       m_act[l:] += a[:-l]
       resp[:n-l] += a[:n-l] * a[l:]
       resp[l:] += a[l:] * a[:-l]
    # source = argmax resp
    # target candidates: order = np.argsort(m_act) maybe; to avoid low mass? m_act low means low neighbor mass, good. Also include random.
```
But resp could be zero if top lags no products? source argmax may pick 0. okay.

Target candidates: If we always pick min m_act, likely same. Add random. Ensure target != source. Also maybe exclude the paired endpoint j? If move from source to j, changes product within peak; could be good if source>>j? But often target same endpoint just transfers mass within pair, product decreases only if source large? Actually if source=s, target=j (other endpoint of max product), moving δ: product term δ(a_s-a_j)-δ^2; if a_s>a_j, reduces peak pair, but other effects. It could be beneficial. Don't exclude.

Factions: [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5] of source mass. If source mass small, fractions small; if source mass large, 0.5 huge. Use maybe `deltas = [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.25,0.5]*a_s`.

Implementation `_local_search`:
```
def _local_search(a, deadline, rng, name, eval_every=50, Ractive=4, ntargets=8):
  a = _prepare(a); n=len(a)
  C = _autocorr_pos(a)[1:]
  C = np.maximum(C,0)
  peak = C.max()
  actual = safe_eval(a)
  best_a=a.copy(); best_actual=actual; best_proxy=peak
  if not finite actual: actual = 2*n*peak
  buf_gs=np.zeros(n-1); buf_gt=np.zeros(n-1); buf_newC=np.zeros(n-1)
  no_improve=0
  while time < deadline:
     top = np.argsort(C)[-Ractive:] + 1
     m_act = np.zeros(n); resp = np.zeros(n)
     for l in top: ...
     source = int(np.argmax(resp)) if resp.max()>0 else int(np.argmax(a))
     # target candidates: low m_act
     # To avoid same every time, take order = np.argsort(m_act); first 8 + random 4
     order = np.argsort(m_act)
     targets = [order[i] for i in range(8)] + randoms
     # Also include source's high neighbor? maybe.
     best_trial = None; best_trial_peak=peak; best_source=source
     # We should also consider different sources? The argmax resp source maybe not optimal; try top 3 resp sources.
     sources = np.argsort(resp)[-3:][::-1] plus source
     for s in sources:
        _coord_grad_for_lags(a,s,buf_gs)
        for t in targets:
           if t==s: continue
           _coord_grad_for_lags(a,t,buf_gt)
           v = buf_gt - buf_gs  # allocate?
           d=abs(t-s)
           for frac in fracs:
              delta = a[s]*frac
              if delta<=0: continue
              buf_newC[:] = C
              buf_newC += delta*v
              if d>=1: buf_newC[d-1] -= delta*delta
              # maybe ensure no negative large; peak = max(buf_newC)
              p = float(np.max(buf_newC))
              if p < best_trial_peak - 1e-15:
                  best_trial_peak=p; store deltas, s,t
     if best_trial and best_trial_peak < peak - 1e-15:
         apply move: a[s]-=delta; a[t]+=delta; clip; normalize?
         # Normalizing sum? already sum 1. Due floating, normalize.
         C = buf_newC.copy() (clipped)
         peak = best_trial_peak; recompute? maybe set
         a[s] = max(a[s]-delta,0); a[t] += delta; a = np.maximum(a, floor); a/=a.sum()
         # Recompute C? Not necessary but due normalization after move changes C slightly (sum constant if normalized same; if we normalize, all values scale; C scales, but we didn't include scaling in move). We moved exact amount preserving sum, then normalize due round; could alter C by relative 1+eps. Since C was for old sum1 and new sum may be 1 within 1e-16; no need. But to be safe, after move if sum not exactly 1, normalize and scale C by (sum_old/sum_new)^2. However sum should remain 1. We can track.
         if no_improve... actual eval if proxy improvement
     else:
         no_improve +=1
         if no_improve > 20:
             # perturb: small noise with best_a? a = 0.98 best_a + 0.02 random; normalize; recompute C; peak might increase; allow exploration but keep best.
             a = ...
             C = _autocorr_pos(a)[1:]; peak=...
             no_improve=0
  return best_a,best_actual
```

Important: `_coord_grad_for_lags` modifies buffer and zero fills. If we compute `v = buf_gt - buf_gs`, this allocates new array. Could instead in delta loop use `buf_newC = C + delta*(gt - gs)`; `gt-gs` alloc. We can preallocate `buf_v` and `np.subtract(buf_gt, buf_gs, out=buf_v)`. Then `buf_newC[:] = C; buf_newC += delta*buf_v`. `delta*buf_v` alloc; to avoid, `buf_newC += buf_v; buf_newC *= delta?` Need C each time. Could `np.add(C, delta*buf_v, out=buf_newC)` still alloc delta*buf_v? There is ufunc with `out` and scalar multiplication? `np.multiply(buf_v, delta, out=tmp)`. Simpler okay; arrays 6k small.

But for many candidates, allocation overhead. We'll write moderately efficient.

Potential issue: if we normalize a after move, C update not exact due scaling. Since we move preserving sum: a[s]-=δ, a[t]+=δ, sum unchanged exactly in real arithmetic; floating sum may differ ~1e-16. Normalizing by sum will scale all by ~1; C should scale by factor^2. If we force normalize, the C we set from update is for unnormalized but effectively same sum. To be safe, don't normalize except if sum deviates >1e-12. We can just `a = np.maximum(a, floor)`? Setting small values? If a[s] becomes negative due δ exactly, ensure δ = min(delta, a[s]*0.999999). Then nonnegative no need clip. Sum remains 1. But floor initialization? If a has floor, no change. We can skip normalization in local moves.

In mirror update, normalization changes sum; C recomputed via FFT.

Exact C update when we add floor? We won't alter zeros. Good.

**Implementation specifics**:

`_next_pow2`:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()
```
But if m=0 return1.

`_prepare_array(seq, n=None)`:
```
def _prepare_array(seq, n=None, floor=1e-12):
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if n is not None:
        arr = arr[:n]
    if arr.size == 0:
        if n is None: n=1024
        arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    if arr.size == 0: arr = np.ones(1)
    s = arr.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
        arr = np.ones(arr.size) / arr.size
    else:
        arr = arr / s
    arr = np.maximum(arr, floor) # but this makes sum slightly >1; normalize.
    s = arr.sum()
    if s > 0: arr = arr/s
    return arr
```
Using floor for all coordinates may bias to uniform; but floor tiny 1e-12 relative sum? Sum increases by n*1e-12 ~6e-9; negligible. But if some original zeros, floor adds mass. Could use `1e-15`? Double safe. For mirror log, use floor inside update, not in stored? We can keep zeros; mirror update uses `a + 1e-300`. If we want growth from zeros, add `1e-12`? If zeros are intentional, maybe no need. But for exploration, small floor okay. However for exact local C, floor mass contributes tiny C ~ n*(1e-12*max) ~1e-11, below peak 1e-4, negligible. Fine.

But if actual objective includes very small side lags? target 1e-4, floor effects 1e-11 negligible.

`_safe_eval(a)`:
```
def _safe_eval(a):
    try:
        if hasattr(a, 'tolist'):
             vals = [float(x) for x in a.tolist()]
        else:
             vals = [float(x) for x in a]
        if not vals: return np.inf
        sc = evaluate_sequence(vals)
        sc = float(sc)
        if np.isfinite(sc): return sc
        return np.inf
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
```
This list conversion each eval O(n). Fine.

`_fft_autocorr_pos`:
```
def _fft_autocorr_pos(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 1:
       return np.array([a[0]*a[0]], dtype=float)
    M = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    ac = np.fft.irfft(F*F.conjugate()? Actually |F|^2: `F * np.conj(F)`. For real rfft, use `F * F.conj()`.
    ac = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), M)[:n]
    ac = np.maximum(ac, 0.0)  # nonnegative
    # Note due to numerical, c0 maybe.
    return ac
```
Need `np.conj(F)` maybe. `np.abs(F)**2` slower but okay. Use `F * F.conj()`.

`_side_max(a, ac=None)`:
```
def _side_max(a, ac=None):
    if ac is None: ac=_fft_autocorr_pos(a)
    if len(ac) <=1: return 0.0
    return float(np.max(ac[1:]))
```

`_side_gradient(a, c, T)`:
- Input c length n.
```
def _side_gradient(a, c, T):
    n=len(a); L=n-1
    side = c[1:]
    peak = float(side.max()) if L>0 else 0.0
    if L == 0 or peak <= 1e-300:
        return np.zeros(n), 0.0
    if T is None or T <= 1e-300:
        p = np.zeros(L); p[int(np.argmax(side))]=1.0
    else:
        z = side - peak
        # avoid underflow: z/T; if T tiny, underflow for many, ok
        p = np.exp(z / T)
        s = p.sum()
        if s <=0: p[int(np.argmax(side))]=1.0
        else: p /= s
    # gradient kernel M
    M = 1 << (3*n - 2).bit_length()
    center = n - 1
    kernel = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
    # set symmetric. Need efficient vectorized:
    # indices_plus = center + np.arange(1,n)
    idx = np.arange(1, L+1, dtype=np.int64)
    kernel[center + idx] = p
    kernel[center - idx] = p
    # center zero implicit
    F_a = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    F_k = np.fft.rfft(kernel, M)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(F_a * F_k, M).real
    m = conv[center:center+n]
    m = np.maximum(m, 0.0)
    return m, peak
```
Potential memory allocate kernel each iteration. M for n=6121: 32768; okay. But repeated allocation 1000s might be ok. We can optimize by preallocating? Pass? Not necessary. But top-level no closures; could have global cache? Not.

We can reduce M: The convolution of a (n) and kernel (2n-1) linear length 3n-2. If we only need m_j for j=0..n-1, do we need full linear no wrap? If M < 3n-2, wrap from high-index kernel terms may contaminate? The kernel has nonzero indices 0..2n-2. a indices 0..n-1. Circular conv at l=center+j (0..n-1) sums a[t] kernel[(center+j-t) mod M]. We need kernel index = center+j-t for terms t in [0,n-1]. If center+j-t could be negative or >=M causing wrap. Range t: min center+j - (n-1) = (n-1)+j-(n-1)=j ≥0; max center+j -0 = 2n-2+j? Wait for j up to n-1, max index = 2n-2+j up to 3n-3. But kernel nonzero only 0..2n-2. If circular M smaller than 3n-2, for large j some kernel index >=M wraps and could pick nonzero kernel at low index, causing contamination. If M >= center+n + max? To avoid any wrap of relevant kernel indices, we need M > max index = center + (n-1) = 2n-2? Actually t min? Let's re-evaluate: Output index l = center + j. For t in 0..n-1, kernel index k = (l - t) mod M. Without mod, range k = l-t. l max = 2n-2, t min 0 => k max 2n-2. l min = n-1, t max n-1 => k min 0. So k range 0..2n-2, exactly kernel support. Ah I mistakenly added j: l = center+j max n-1 + (n-1)=2n-2. Yes because center=n-1, not center+n? For m_j we need conv[center+j] with j 0..n-1 => max 2n-2. Min n-1. Thus k range <=2n-2. If M >2n-2 (or M >=2n-1) maybe no wrap? For circular conv, if k computed negative? min k = n-1 - (n-1)=0. So all k in [0,2n-2], within M if M>=2n-1. But wait for l=n-1+j and t could be >l? no min 0. So if M >=2n-1, no wrap at all for output indices center..center+n-1? However circular convolution includes contributions from kernel indices that are k mod M. Since desired k positive <M, no wrap. What about kernel nonzero index k >2n-2? none. Thus M=next_pow2(2n-1) sufficient! Is that true because kernel centered at n-1 and a placed at 0..n-1; output around center. The linear convolution length 3n-2, but central segment of length n does not wrap if M >=2n-1? In circular convolution, missing terms with k from l-t that would be negative? But l-t min 0, no negative. So yes. Need check n=3, M>=5. l=2+j=2..4. t=0..2. k=2..4, etc, no negative. desired kernel index up to 4. M=5. For l=4,j=2,t=0,k=4; ok. t=2,k=2. Good. So gradient M can be same as autocorr M (2n-1) not 3n-2! Great, faster. But we need ensure for output index l = center+j, circular convolution using F_a and F_k length M computes sum_t a[t] kernel[(l-t) mod M]. Since l-t in [j, center+j] (not negative) and <=2n-2. If M >=2n-1, no wrap. Good. Use M = next_pow2(2n-1). For n=6121, M=16384. Nice.

Autocorr also needs M >=2n-1. Good.

So `_side_gradient` can use M=len p? We'll compute same.

But wait kernel support 0..2n-2 length 2n-1. F_k length M. The central segment of linear convolution output l=n-1..2n-2 is in first half, no wrap. Good.

Thus gradient FFT size 16384. Very fast.

`_mirror_update`:
```
def _mirror_update(a, m, eta):
    a_safe = a + 1e-300
    z = m - m.max()
    # If m.max()==0, z=0, new=a
    new = a_safe * np.exp(eta * z)  # z <=0
    # floor
    new = np.maximum(new, 1e-300)
    s = new.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return a.copy()
    new /= s
    return new
```
Maybe use `eta * (z - z.mean())`? Since after normalization constant irrelevant, subtracting max same as not. If we subtract mean, negative values for low m cause exp positive >1, high <1. Equivalent to subtracting max? Not exactly: subtract mean shifts all; after normalization, relative same as subtract any constant! Yes `exp(-eta(m-c))` normalized independent of c? Sum changes but normalized: new_i ∝ a_i exp(-eta m_i); constant c cancels in normalization. So using z = m - m.max() is fine.

**Temperature schedule in optimize**:
We need estimate progress. Use `job_start = time.monotonic()`, `job_budget = deadline - job_start`. In loop:
```
elapsed = time.monotonic() - job_start
progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, job_budget))
tau = tau_start * (tau_end / tau_start) ** progress
```
But if we implement loop until deadline, job_budget fixed. Good.
`T = peak * tau` maybe if tau ~0.35 to 0.005. If peak changes, T changes. If T becomes too small, p one-hot. Good.

Eta schedule: Could also decrease from 0.8 to 0.05. For early broad, high eta. Use `eta_base = 0.9 * (0.03/0.9)**progress`, but adaptive backtracking. We can maintain `eta = eta_base`. Simpler:
- `tau` as above.
- `eta = 0.8 * (0.05/0.8)**min(1,progress*1.2)`.
Each iteration, try `eta`, `eta/2`, `eta/4`, maybe `eta*2` early? But line search. We can do up to 3 attempts:
   currents = [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25]
   for cand_eta in currents:
       new = mirror; new_peak; if improve accept.
   if none: if peak - best_proxy? Since we only accept improvements, current=best. Try a small random mirror with eta=eta*0.1, or perturb.
If accept, maybe increase eta if improved significantly? We can set `eta = min(1.5, cand_eta*1.1)`. If no, `eta=max(0.01, eta*0.6)`.

But if we only accept proxy improvements, we might become very conservative. Good.

We need `best_proxy` track? Current a is best (we revert if no improve). But for perturbation, current may worsen; keep best copy. In optimize, if perturb worsens, we continue from perturbed to escape; but best remains. At end, return best. During perturbation, maybe many proxy increases; that's okay but could waste. We should limit and revert if too bad? Maybe.

Pseudo optimize:
```
def _optimize_side(a, deadline, rng, name="", trials_per_iter=3, eval_every=20):
   job_start=time.monotonic(); job_budget=max(5.0, deadline-job_start)
   a = _prepare_array(a)
   c = _fft_autocorr_pos(a); peak = _side_max(a,c)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_proxy=peak; best_actual=_safe_eval(a)
   if not finite best_actual: best_actual=2*n*peak
   it=0; no_improve=0
   tau_start=0.4; tau_end=0.003
   eta_start=0.9; eta_end=0.04
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
       it +=1
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-job_start)/job_budget)
       tau = tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**progress
       eta = eta_start * (eta_end/eta_start)**progress
       m, peak = _side_gradient(a, c, peak*tau if n>1 else 0)
       # if m all zero, break
       maxm = m.max()
       if maxm <= 1e-300: break
       accepted=False
       candidates = [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25]
       # apply mild scaling to m: z already inside
       for ce in candidates:
           new_a = _mirror_update(a, m, ce)
           new_c = _fft_autocorr_pos(new_a)
           new_peak = float(np.max(new_c[1:])) if len(new_c)>1 else 0.0
           if new_peak < peak - 1e-14:
               a=new_a; c=new_c; peak=new_peak; accepted=True
               eta = min(1.5, ce*1.15)
               no_improve=0
               break
       if not accepted:
           no_improve +=1
           eta = max(0.01, eta*0.6)
           if no_improve > 8:
               # perturb from best_a to escape? maybe blend best and random
               if rng is not None:
                   mix = 0.05 + 0.05*rng.random()
                   noise = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.05)
                   a = (1-mix)*best_a + mix*noise
                   a = np.maximum(a,0); a/=a.sum()
                   c = _fft_autocorr_pos(a); peak=_side_max(a,c)
               else:
                   break
               no_improve=0
       # evaluation
       if it % eval_every == 0 or peak < best_proxy*(1-1e-6):
           sc = _safe_eval(a)
           if sc < best_actual:
              best_actual=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_proxy=peak
           if peak < best_proxy:
              best_proxy=peak; best_a=a.copy()
```

**Top-level helper `_make_starts`**:
```
def _make_starts(n, rng, given=None):
    starts=[]
    if given is not None:
       starts.append(('given', given.copy()))
    starts.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)))
    j=np.arange(n); c=(n-1)/2
    starts.append(('u1', 1.0 + 1.0*((j-c)/c)**2))
    starts.append(('u4', 1.0 + 4.0*((j-c)/c)**2))
    starts.append(('bimod', np.exp(-((j-0.28*n)/(0.07*n))**2)+np.exp(-((j-0.72*n)/(0.07*n))**2)))
    starts.append(('rand_dirichlet', rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.1)))
    starts.append(('rand_exp', rng.exponential(1.0,n)))
    # binary random with m=int(1.2*sqrt(n))? maybe continuous
    m=max(2,int(1.2*np.sqrt(n)))
    b=np.zeros(n); idx=rng.choice(n,m,replace=False); b[idx]=1; starts.append(('bin_sparse', b))
    # maybe sine low freq
    starts.append(('sine', 1.0+0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*j/n)))
    return starts
```
But Dirichlet alpha 0.1 length 6000 might be expensive? okay. Exponential ok.

Detailed `_local_search` code:
```
def _local_search(a, deadline, rng, name="", eval_every=30, max_moves=100000):
    a = _prepare_array(a)
    n=len(a); L=n-1
    c_full = _fft_autocorr_pos(a)
    C = c_full[1:].copy()
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    peak = float(np.max(C)) if L>0 else 0.0
    best_act = _safe_eval(a)
    best_act_a = a.copy()
    if not np.isfinite(best_act):
         best_act = 2.0*n*peak
    best_proxy = peak
    best_proxy_a = a.copy()
    move_count=0; no_improve=0; last_recompute=0
    buf_gs = np.zeros(L); buf_gt = np.zeros(L); buf_v = np.zeros(L); buf_newC = np.zeros(L)
    fracs = (0.01,0.03,0.08,0.16,0.3,0.5)
    Ractive = 4
    n_target = 8
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
       move_count +=1
       # ensure C valid
       if move_count == 1 or (move_count % 500 == 0) or (C.min() < -1e-12):
           c_full = _fft_autocorr_pos(a); C = c_full[1:]; C = np.maximum(C,0); peak = float(np.max(C)) if L>0 else 0.0
       # top lags
       if L==0: break
       top_idx = np.argsort(C)[-Ractive:]
       m_act = np.zeros(n); resp = np.zeros(n)
       for idx in top_idx:
           lag = int(idx)+1
           if lag >= n: continue
           m_act[:n-lag] += a[lag:]
           m_act[lag:] += a[:-lag]
           resp[:n-lag] += a[:n-lag] * a[lag:]
           resp[lag:] += a[lag:] * a[:-lag]
       if resp.max() <= 0:
           # no contribution? perturb
           ...
       sources = np.argsort(resp)[-3:][::-1]
       targets_ord = np.argsort(m_act)
       # build target list
       targets=[]
       for pos in targets_ord[:n_target]: targets.append(int(pos))
       # add random
       for _ in range(4):
           targets.append(int(rng.integers(0,n)))
       best_trial_peak = peak
       best trial vars
       for s in sources:
           s=int(s)
           _coord_grad_lags(a,s,buf_gs)
           for t in targets:
               t=int(t)
               if t==s: continue
               _coord_grad_lags(a,t,buf_gt)
               np.subtract(buf_gt, buf_gs, out=buf_v)
               d=abs(t-s)
               # If source mass zero skip
               if a[s] <= 1e-15: continue
               # Optional: if target same m high? not.
               for frac in fracs:
                   delta = a[s]*frac
                   if delta <= 1e-18: continue
                   # quick scalar upper bound? newC = C + ...; max maybe
                   np.add(C, 0.0, out=buf_newC) ???
```

```
def _apply_coord_move(a, C, s, t, delta, ...):
    if delta > a[s]: delta = a[s]
    a = a.copy()? modify in place
    a[s] -= delta; a[t] += delta
    # a nonneg; no normalize
    _coord_grad_lags(original a?) Need gradient using original a before move. We must compute before modifying. So store original a_s/a_t. In local search, compute v before. If applying, use precomputed v? We can recompute with original a before changing. In loop, original a unchanged. At apply, ensure `buf_v` from best? It may be overwritten. We can recompute:
       _coord_grad_lags(a,t,gt); _coord_grad_lags(a,s,gs); v=gt-gs
       newC = C + delta*v; newC[d-1]-=delta*delta
       clip
       a[s]-=delta; a[t]+=delta
       return newC, peak
```

**`_make_starts`**: Need avoid too many. Let's return list of arrays only; we can name print by index. Simpler:
```
def _make_starts(n, rng, given):
    starts=[]
    if given is not None: starts.append(given.copy())
    starts.append(np.ones(n))
    j=np.arange(n,float)
    c=(n-1)/2
    if c>0:
       starts.append(1+ ((j-c)/c)**2)
       starts.append(1+4*((j-c)/c)**2)
    starts.append(np.exp(-((j-0.3*n)/(0.08*n))**2)+np.exp(-((j-0.7*n)/(0.08*n))**2)+1e-6)
    starts.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.05))
    starts.append(rng.exponential(1.0,n))
    # scaled log-normal? `rng.lognormal(0,0.5,n)`
    starts.append(rng.lognormal(0,0.5,n))
    m=max(2,int(1.2*np.sqrt(n)))
    b=np.zeros(n); idx=rng.choice(n,m,replace=False); b[idx]=1
    starts.append(b)
    # binary low-frequency? `1 + 0.5*sin`
    starts.append(1+0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*j/n))
    return starts
```
Lognormal may have large values; prepare clips 1000 but normalized. Fine.

Algorithm `blackbox_local`:

**Implementing a robust oracle optimizer in code**:

We can use a simpler version of the previous mirror descent but with actual-guided acceptance and test multiple lag sets. This is different enough.

Functions:
- `_lag_gradient(a, lag_indices, weights)` top-level.
- `_generate_mirror_candidates(a, c, eta_list, lag_choices, max_candidates)`.
- `_generate_coordinate_candidates(a, c, max_candidates, rng)`.
- `_evaluate_candidates(candidates, deadline, best_score)` returns best array/score. No lambda.

But candidates arrays many copies length n; memory okay if small.

`_lag_gradient`:
```
def _lag_gradient(a, lags, weights):
    n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
    for lag, w in zip(lags, weights):
      lag=int(lag)
      if lag==0:
         g += 2.0*w*a
      else:
         g[:n-lag] += 2.0*w*a[lag:]
         g[lag:] += 2.0*w*a[:n-lag]
    return g
```
Works for positive lags; negative symmetric same.

Candidate generation from best a:
- `lags_choices` list of (name, lags, weights). We can build outside.
  - center: [(0,1)]
  - side_top1: [(k1,1)] where k1 argmax c[1:]
  - side_top3: top 3 side uniform weights.
  - side_top5: top5 proportional to c (or normalized).
  - mixed: [(0,0.5)] + top3 side.
  - random side: choose R random lags with nonzero c? maybe.
- For each choice, compute g. If g.max()<=g.min(), skip (uniform gradient no change). Candidate for eta in eta_list: `a_new = _mirror_update(a, g, eta)`.

**Let's design `_oracle_refine` in detail**:

Inputs: `a` (np array normalized), `deadline`, `rng`, `name`, `max_candidates=6`, `eval_every? no, every candidate eval`.

It maintains:
- `best_a`, `best_score` (actual).
- `cur_a`, `cur_score` (current exploration state, may be worse).
- `it`, `stuck`.

Loop until deadline:
1. Ensure `cur_a` finite; compute `c_full = _fft_autocorr_pos(cur_a)` (positive lags). If n<=1 break.
2. Generate candidate arrays (not evaluating) from current `cur_a` and `c_full`:
   - Mirror candidates using lag choices.
   - Coordinate candidates using top side lags and uniformization.
3. Evaluate up to `max_candidates` best gauged by quick proxies. Pick candidate with lowest actual.
4. If any actual < cur_score - 1e-12 (or < best_score - ...?), set `cur_a = cand`, `cur_score = sc`; if sc < best_score update best. `stuck=0`.
5. If no improvement, maybe try a random perturbation candidate and evaluate; if perturbation actual lower? unlikely. If stuck > 20: create perturbation from best_a, evaluate as new cur (even if worse), `stuck=0`.
6. To avoid too many evals if eval slow, maybe after each iteration check time.

Candidate generation implementation:

`_generate_mirror_candidates(a, c, rng, dataset)`:
- `n=len(a); side=c[1:]` if n>1.
- Build lag choices:
   - `['center']: lags=[0]`
   - side top: `top_side_idx = np.argsort(side)[-3:][::-1]+1` (if any positive). If side all zero? then side trivial; but spike candidate likely.
   - choice1: lags=top_side_idx[:1], weights=[1]
   - choice2: lags=top_side_idx[:3], weights=1/len
   - choice3: lags=top5, weights=side values normalized.
   - mixed: lags=[0]+top3, weights=[0.5, 0.5/3...] maybe.
   - random: choose 3 lags from top 20 or random via rng; but maybe not.
- Compute candidate states:
   eta_list=(0.15,0.4,1.0)
   For each choice, g = _lag_gradient; if g.max()-g.min() small skip; for eta in eta_list: cand = _mirror_update(a,g,eta). Add.
- Also a plain multiplicative update with **sum of all side lags uniform** (p uniform) maybe: g_side_all = convolution with uniform side? Too expensive; skip.

We need avoid duplicates; can add as tuple.

`_generate_coordinate_candidates(a, c, rng, max_count)`:

- Side moves: for top2 side lags: compute contribution products; choose source index among endpoint of max product (larger mass), targets: lowest neighbor gradient (for active lags) maybe 2; fractions. Similar local but simplified.

Selection K:
```
def _select_candidate_indices(data, K):
    m=len(data)
    if m<=K: return list(range(m))
    # data is list of (full, side, c0, cand)
    chosen=[]
    # order by side
    side_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[1] for d in data]))
    full_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[0] for d in data]))
    c0_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[2] for d in data]))
    for idx in list(side_order[:K//2]) + list(full_order[:K//2]) + list(c0_order[:K//2]):
       if 0<=idx<m and idx not in chosen: chosen.append(idx)
       if len(chosen)>=K: break
    return chosen
```

### `_prepare_array`
```
def _prepare_array(seq, n=None, floor=1e-12):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if n is not None:
        if arr.size >= n:
            arr = arr[:n]
        else:
            # pad with zeros? if given shorter
            tmp = np.zeros(n); tmp[:arr.size]=arr; arr=tmp
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(1024)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.sum() <= 1e-12:
        arr = np.ones(arr.size)
    arr = arr / arr.sum()
    arr = np.maximum(arr, floor)
    arr = arr / arr.sum()
    return arr
```
If input height_sequence_1 has negative, clip. Good.

### `_safe_eval`
```
def _safe_eval(arr):
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).tolist()]
        if not vals: return np.inf
        sc = evaluate_sequence(vals)
        sc = float(sc)
        if np.isfinite(sc): return sc
        return np.inf
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
```
This creates np array each time; okay.

### `_fft_autocorr_pos`
```
def _fft_autocorr_pos(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n<=1:
       return np.array([float(a[0]*a[0])], dtype=np.float64)
    M = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, M)
    ac = np.fft.irfft(F * F.conj(), M)[:n]
    ac = np.asarray(ac, dtype=np.float64)
    ac[ac<0]=0.0
    return ac
```
For n=1, bit_length? fine.

### `_gradient_for_lags`
```
def _gradient_for_lags(a, lags, weights):
    n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for lag,w in zip(lags,weights):
       k=int(lag); w=float(w)
       if w==0: continue
       if k==0:
          g += 2.0*w*a
       elif k < n:
          g[:n-k] += 2.0*w*a[k:]
          g[k:] += 2.0*w*a[:n-k]
    return g
```
If weights not normalized, okay.

### `_mirror_update`
```
def _mirror_update(a, g, eta):
    gmax = float(g.max())
    if gmax <= 0: return a.copy()
    z = g - gmax
    # clip z to avoid exp overflow (z<=0)
    safe_a = a + 1e-300
    new = safe_a * np.exp(eta * z)
    new = np.maximum(new, 0.0)
    s = new.sum()
    if s <=0: return a.copy()
    new = new / s
    new = np.maximum(new, 1e-12)
    new = new / new.sum()
    return new
```
Since z<=0, exp underflow okay.

### `_candidate_proxy`
```
def _candidate_proxy(cand):
    c = _fft_autocorr_pos(cand)
    c0 = float(c[0])
    full = float(np.max(c)) if len(c) else 0.0
    side = float(np.max(c[1:])) if len(c)>1 else 0.0
    return full, side, c0
```
This does FFT. Could be used many times. We can optimize by only for selected? Actually to select we need proxy. Candidate count small.

### `_generate_mirror_candidates`
Return list of arrays. Need `c` positive lags from current.
```
def _generate_mirror_candidates(a, c, rng=None):
    n=len(a); out=[]
    if n<=1: return out
    side=c[1:]
    eta_list=(0.15,0.35,0.8)
    # center
    g=_gradient_for_lags(a,[0],[1.0])
    for e in eta_list: out.append(_mirror_update(a,g,e))
    # top side
    if side.size>0 and side.max()>1e-300:
       order=np.argsort(side)
       top=order[-5:][::-1]+1
       # top1
       g1=_gradient_for_lags(a,[int(top[0])],[1.0])
       for e in eta_list: out.append(_mirror_update(a,g1,e))
       # top3 uniform
       if top.size>=1:
          k3=int(min(3,len(top)))
          g3=_gradient_for_lags(a,[int(x) for x in top[:k3]], [1.0/k3]*k3)
          for e in eta_list: out.append(_mirror_update(a,g3,e))
       # weighted top5
       if top.size>0:
          vec=side[top-1].astype(float)
          w=vec/ vec.sum()
          gw=_gradient_for_lags(a,[int(x) for x in top], list(w))
          for e in eta_list: out.append(_mirror_update(a,gw,e))
       # mixed center+side
       if top.size>0:
          k=int(min(2,len(top)))
          lags=[0]+[int(x) for x in top[:k]]
          weights=[0.5]+[0.5/k]*k
          gm=_gradient_for_lags(a,lags,weights)
          for e in eta_list: out.append(_mirror_update(a,gm,e))
    # remove duplicates? Not necessary; proxy selection may evaluate duplicates. Could skip if too many.
    return out
```
Maybe too many candidates: center3 + top1 3 + top3 3 + weighted3 + mixed3 =15. Fine.

### `_generate_coordinate_candidates`
```
def _generate_coordinate_candidates(a, c, rng=None):
    n=len(a); out=[]
    if n<=2: return out
    side=c[1:]
    fracs=(0.02,0.08,0.2)
    # uniformization
    order_mass=np.argsort(a)
    highs=order_mass[-3:][::-1]
    lows=order_mass[:3]
    for s in highs:
      for t in lows:
        if s==t: continue
        for fr in fracs:
           cand=a.copy(); delta=a[s]*fr
           if delta<=0: continue
           cand[s]-=delta; cand[t]+=delta
           cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0); ssum=cand.sum()
           if ssum>0: cand/=ssum
           out.append(cand)
    # side top moves
    if side.size>0 and side.max()>1e-300:
       top_lags=(np.argsort(side)[-2:][::-1]+1).astype(int)
       for lag in top_lags:
          inten=n-lag
          if inten<=0: continue
          prod=a[:inten]*a[lag:inten+lag] # a[lag:]
          if prod.size==0: continue
          idx=int(np.argmax(prod))
          i=idx; j=idx+lag
          src = i if a[i]>=a[j] else j
          # target low neighbor for this lag
          neighbor=np.zeros(n)
          neighbor[:n-lag] += a[lag:]
          neighbor[lag:] += a[:n-lag]
          # exclude src and maybe the other endpoint? include
          neigh=neighbor.copy()
          neigh[src]=np.inf
          # maybe set endpoint? include low; if endpoint low and src high, moving to endpoint can reduce product.
          targets=np.argsort(neigh)[:3]
          for t in targets:
             if t==src or a[s]==0: continue
             for fr in (0.05,0.2):
                cand=a.copy(); delta=a[src]*fr
                cand[src]-=delta; cand[t]+=delta
                cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0); ss=cand.sum()
                if ss>0: cand/=ss
                out.append(cand)
    # limit output length maybe 40
    if len(out)>40: out=out[:40]
    return out
```
The `a[lag:inten+lag]` should be `a[lag:]` length n-lag. Good.
Candidate count: uniform 3*3*3=27; side 2*3*2=12; total39. Good.

### `_select_candidates`
```
def _select_candidates(candidates, K=8):
    if not candidates: return []
    if len(candidates)<=K:
       return list(range(len(candidates)))
    data=[]
    for idx,cand in enumerate(candidates):
       full,side,c0 = _candidate_proxy(cand)
       data.append((idx, full, side, c0))
    # orders
    side_idx = data sorted by side? no lambda.
    side_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[2] for d in data]))  # data tuple idx, full, side, c0 => side index 2
    full_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[1] for d in data]))
    c0_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[3] for d in data]))
    # combined = full+side
    comb_order = np.argsort(np.array([d[1] + d[2] for d in data]))
    chosen=[]
    for orders in (side_order, full_order, c0_order, comb_order):
       for arr_idx in orders:
          idx = int(data[arr_idx][0])
          if idx not in chosen:
             chosen.append(idx)
          if len(chosen)>=K: return chosen
    return chosen
```
But `data[arr_idx][0]` is original candidate idx. Good. No lambda.
Potential duplicates selected; we check.

Candidate count 54, proxy FFT each -> 54*0.003=0.16s per iteration, maybe too much if 1000 iters=160s. Acceptable maybe. But we can reduce candidate count: In `_oracle_refine`, generate mirror 15 + coordinate maybe 12 (reduce uniform fracs). Let's reduce to keep total ~25.

Modify coordinate: fracs 2, targets 2, high/low 2x2*2=8 + side top2*2*2=8 total16. Total31. Proxy 31*0.003=0.09s.

`candidates_per_iter` K=6 actual evals. Proxy cost small.

### `_oracle_refine`
```
def _oracle_refine(a, deadline, rng, name="", max_candidates=6, eval_limit=2000):
    a = _prepare_array(a)
    cur = a.copy()
    cur_score = _safe_eval(cur)
    best = a.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    if not finite cur_score: cur_score = 2*n*max(c)??? Actually can set from proxy but best inf.
    it=0; stuck=0; perturb_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
       it +=1
       c = _fft_autocorr_pos(cur)
       # if current score not finite, evaluate
       if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
           cur_score = _safe_eval(cur)
       # generate candidates
       cands = _generate_mirror_candidates(cur,c,rng)
       if len(cands) < 20: cands += _generate_coordinate_candidates(cur,c,rng)
       # filter candidates too similar? skip
       sel = _select_candidates(cands, max_candidates)
       improved=False
       best_cand_idx=None; best_cand_score=cur_score
       for idx in sel:
           if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0: break
           cand = cands[idx].copy()
           # Ensure valid
           sc = _safe_eval(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score - 1e-12:
              best_cand_score=sc; best_cand_idx=idx
       if best_cand_idx is not None:
           cur = cands[best_cand_idx].copy()
           cur_score = best_cand_score
           if cur_score < best_score:
              best_score=cur_score; best=cur.copy()
           improved=True; stuck=0
       else:
           stuck +=1
           # If long stuck, perturb best and switch current
           if stuck > 15:
              # choose perturbation
              base = best if np.isfinite(best_score) else cur
              mix = 0.05 + 0.1*rng.random()
              noise = rng.normal(1.0, 0.25, size=len(base))
              noise = np.maximum(noise, 0.0)
              if noise.sum() <=0: noise=np.ones(len(base))
              noise /= noise.sum()
              cur = (1.0-mix)*base + mix*noise
              cur = np.maximum(cur,0); cur /= cur.sum()
              cur_score = _safe_eval(cur)
              if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
                  cur = base.copy(); cur_score=best_score
              stuck=0
              perturb_count +=1
           # else maybe try random single coordinate move? We already generated coordinate; no improvement.
       # If current proxy/spike score zero? if best_score <= 1e-12 break
       if best_score <= 1e-12: break
    # final evaluate best
    sc = _safe_eval(best)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score: best_score=sc
    return best, best_score
```

This refines using actual evaluations for a limited number of candidates. It may take many iterations; each iteration 6 evals + proxy. If eval 0.2s, 6*0.2=1.2s, 800 iters=960s. Good.

Need implement `_smooth_side_gradient` top-level. It assumes c positive lags.
```
def _smooth_side_gradient(a, c, T):
   n=len(a); side=c[1:]; peak=float(side.max())
   if side.size==0 or peak<=1e-300: return np.zeros(n)
   if T<=1e-300: p=zeros; p[argmax]=1 else p=exp((side-peak)/T); p/=sum
   M=1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
   center=n-1
   kernel=np.zeros(M)
   idx=np.arange(1,n)
   kernel[center+idx]=p
   kernel[center-idx]=p
   F_a=np.fft.rfft(a,M); F_k=np.fft.rfft(kernel,M)
   conv=np.fft.irfft(F_a*F_k,M)
   m=conv[center:center+n]
   return np.maximum(m,0.0)
```
M size 16384. Good. Add 2-4 candidates. Candidate count increases to 35.

Smooth side gradient may be the best for previous score. It uses side lags; if actual full, center gradient also. If actual unknown, actual eval.

```
def propose_candidate(...):
  t0=monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget-15
  probe=_probe()
  # get main n, given

     spike=make main spike; sc=safe_eval; if sc<1e-6: return spike.tolist
  # initial starts
  starts=[] arrays

     if sc<best_score: update
  # if best_score inf, use uniform

  order=np.argsort(np.array([s if finite else 1e300 for s,_ in start_data]))
  top_start=[start_data[i][1] for i in order[:4]]
  # allocate refine
  if best_score <=1e-12: return best_a

  # refine second if time

  # refine third maybe 0.15

  # final refine on global best with remaining 20

  # symmetrize and random variants maybe

  return best list
```

Let's consider exact code for `_select_candidates` no lambda:
```
def _select_candidates(candidates, current, K):
    # filter distinct
    fil=[]
    for cand in candidates:
        if cand.size != current.size: continue
        d = float(np.sum(np.abs(cand - current)))
        if d > 1e-9:
            fil.append(cand)
    if not fil: return []
    if len(fil) <= K:
        return list(range(len(fil)))
    # compute proxies
    prox = np.zeros((len(fil),4))
    for i,c in enumerate(fil):
        ca = _fft_autocorr_pos(c)
        full = float(np.max(ca))
        side = float(np.max(ca[1:])) if ca.size>1 else 0.0
        c0 = float(ca[0])
        prox[i] = [full, side, c0, full+side]
    orders = (np.argsort(prox[:,2]), np.argsort(prox[:,1]), np.argsort(prox[:,3]), np.argsort(prox[:,0]))
    chosen=[]
    for o in orders:
        for idx in o:
            idx=int(idx)
            if idx not in chosen:
                chosen.append(idx)
            if len(chosen)>=K: return chosen
    return chosen
```
`prox` row values float. `orders` tuple of np arrays. Good.

If proxies have NaN? FFT on finite nonnegative no. If candidate has huge? normalized.

```
if given_raw is not None:
```

```
def _final_list(arr):
   arr=np.asarray(arr,dtype=float).ravel()
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0)
   arr=np.maximum(arr,1e-12)
   arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
   s=arr.sum()
   if s<=0: arr=np.ones(len(arr)); s=arr.sum()
   arr=arr/s
   return [float(x) for x in arr.tolist()]
```

```
def _make_start_arrays(n, rng, given):
    arrs=[]
    if given is not None: arrs.append(given.copy())
    arrs.append(np.ones(n))
    s=np.zeros(n); s[0]=1; arrs.append(s)
    t=np.zeros(n); t[0]=1; t[n//2]=1; arrs.append(t)
    try: arrs.append(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n)*0.05))
    except Exception: arrs.append(rng.exponential(1.0,n))
    j=np.arange(n,float)
    if n>1:
       x=j/(n-1)-0.5; arrs.append(1.0+4.0*x*x)
    if n>1:
       arrs.append(np.exp(-((j-0.28*n)/(0.07*n))**2)+np.exp(-((j-0.72*n)/(0.07*n))**2)+1e-6)
    arrs.append(1.0+0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*j/n))
    return arrs
```

```
def _generate_mirror_candidates(a,c):
    out=[]
    n=len(a)
    if n<=1: return out
    g0=_gradient_for_lags(a,[0],[1.0])
    out.append(_mirror_update(a,g0,0.35))
    out.append(_mirror_update(a,g0,0.9))
    side=c[1:]
    if side.size>0 and side.max()>1e-300:
        order=np.argsort(side)
        top=order[-5:][::-1]+1
        k1=int(top[0]); g1=...; out.append(_mirror_update(a,g1,0.5))
        k=min(3,len(top)); g3=...; out.append(_mirror_update(a,g3,0.5))
        # mixed
        lags=[0]+[int(x) for x in top[:2]]; weights=[0.5,0.25,0.25]? if 2 side weights sum0.5.
        gm=...; out.append(_mirror_update(a,gm,0.5))
        # smooth
        peak=float(side.max())
        for tau in (0.15,0.03):
            g=_smooth_side_gradient(a,c,peak*tau)
            if g.max()>0:
                out.append(_mirror_update(a,g,0.3))
                out.append(_mirror_update(a,g,0.8))
    return out
```

```
def _generate_coordinate_candidates(a,c):
   n=len(a); out=[]
   if n<=2: return out
   fracs=(0.05,0.2)
   # uniform top2/bottom2
   om=np.argsort(a); highs=om[-2:][::-1]; lows=om[:2]
   for s in highs:
     for t in lows:
       if s==t: continue
       for fr in fracs: add
   side=c[1:]
   if side.size>0 and side.max()>1e-300:
      top_lags=(np.argsort(side)[-2:][::-1]+1).astype(int)
      for lag in top_lags:
         inten=n-lag; if inten<=0 continue
         prod=a[:inten]*a[lag:]
         idx=int(np.argmax(prod)); src = idx if a[idx]>=a[idx+lag] else idx+lag
         neigh=np.zeros(n); neigh[:n-lag]+=a[lag:]; neigh[lag:]+=a[:n-lag]
         neigh[src]=np.inf
         neigh[idx]=? maybe keep
         targets=np.argsort(neigh)[:2]
         for t in targets:
            if t==src: continue
            for fr in (0.05,0.2): add
   return out
```

If evaluator side, stwo ~48.5 <72.75 false. If full 97 >72.75 true. Good.

**If actual evaluator side with spike trivial, we return early before mode.

**If probe uniform inf? mode_full maybe inf? `np.isfinite(stwo)` check.

**Potential overflow in `np.convolve` for n=97 no.

**Now, let's write final code in fenced block. Ensure no analysis. Use imports only numpy, time.

Double-check `_smooth_side_gradient` M: If n=1, side empty return zeros before. For n>1, M=next_pow2(2*n-1). Use `_next_pow2`.

`_next_pow2` implementation:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```
For m=2, (1).bit_length=1 ->2; m=3, (2).bit_length=2 ->4; m=4, (3).bit_length=2 ->4. okay. Could be 1<<m.bit_length? For m=4 gives8? Let's test: (m-1).bit_length is correct for next power of two >=m. For m=1 returns? we handle.

`_fft_autocorr_pos` for n=6121 m=12241? 2n-1=12241. bit_length(12240)=14? 2^13=8192, 2^14=16384. Good.

`_smooth_side_gradient` M=1<<... maybe 16384. Kernel length M. Indices center+idx up to 12240 <16384.

Candidate `_select_candidates` uses `_fft_autocorr_pos` for each candidate; if candidate large, okay. It creates `prox = np.zeros((len(fil),4))`; if len 0. Good.

**Potential bug: `_select_candidates` orders tuple; if prox has NaN, argsort puts NaN at end maybe. Safe.

**Potential bug: In `_oracle_refine`, after accepting a candidate, `c` not updated until next loop; fine.

**Potential bug: `_generate_coordinate_candidates`: `prod = a[:inten] * a[lag:]`; if `a` length n, `a[lag:]` length n-lag. `inten=n-lag`, okay. `idx+lag` within n. Good.

**Potential bug: In perturb, `noise = rng.normal(1.0,0.25,size=len(base))`; if len(base)=0? no. `noise = np.maximum(noise,0.0)`; if sum=0, ones. Good.

**Potential bug: `_oracle_refine` if `cur_score` is inf and `best_score` inf, perturb base if best inf uses cur. Then candidate eval maybe. If all inf, final best_a maybe a. In propose final fallback uniform if best_score inf.

**`_final_list` floors all to 1e-12; if array length huge and all zeros? set ones.

**Now final. Ensure code is between ```python and ```. No extra text. Done.
